2026 NFL trade deadline tracker: Vikings reunite with Kirk Cousins as in-season moves wrap up

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

A steady stream of blockbuster moves in advance of the 2026 NFL trade deadline redistributed some of the league's top talent at numerous positions. A grand total of 18 in-season trades made the 2026 campaign the fourth-most active year of player movement and defined where teams stand in the playoff race and the hunt for the No. 1 overall pick in the 2027 draft.

Teams including the Chicago Bears and Jacksonville Jaguars went all-in ahead of the deadline when they spent premier draft capital on defensive standouts like Jeffery Simmons and Jordan Davis. Those swaps made the Super Bowl contenders two of the deadline's biggest winners.

On the other hand, the Indianapolis Colts emerged as by far the most aggressive sellers when they parted with Quenton Nelson, DeForest Buckner, Keenan Allen and Josh Downs. That dichotomy shows exactly how teams used Nov. 10 as a pivot point in their quest for either a championship or a jumpstart in the rebuilding process.

A bevy of big-name trade candidates stood still when the deadline passed. The Colts' fire sale did not extend to Jonathan Taylor or Daniel Jones, while defenders like Khalil Mack, Will McDonald and Budda Baker remained with their respective teams, too.

Below is every in-season trade processed in advance of the NFL's 2026 deadline.

Just over a month ago, the Ravens pulled off the league's third in-season trade to acquire Cam Jurgens. The move landed them a long-term solution at center following Tyler Linderbaum's exit in free agency. Now they have another center on the roster. Neuzil was the Falcons' starter at that position this season but appears poised to take on a backup role for the Ravens unless he moves to guard (a position he played to an extent in Atlanta and exclusively in college). Olaivavega Ioane is locked in at right guard, so it will come down to John Simpson and Neuzil on the left side. The other will head to the bench as the primary interior offensive line reserve.

The Falcons will not walk away empty-handed, as Vorhees brings starting experience at guard. There will be some shifting around to do on that offensive line.

Kansas City owns the 10th-best passing offense in the NFL but is more reliant on yards after the catch than any team in football. Additionally, wide receivers accounted for only 46.0% of the Chiefs' receiving yards through Week 9, the second-lowest rate, behind only the Raiders at 36.8%, according to Next Gen Stats. This team needed another weapon for Patrick Mahomes and secured one just minutes before the trade deadline in the form of Downs. Although he is not much of a field-stretcher, he could free up Rashee Rice and Xavier Worthy or expand his own role now that he is on a new team.

That concludes the Colts' fire sale, bringing the tally to four players traded. All of them were on expiring contracts, so Indianapolis prioritized landing guaranteed draft capital for players likely to leave in free agency, who may or may not have netted compensatory picks. There are now only five healthy wide receivers on the Colts' active roster: Laquon Treadwell, Deion Burks, Ashton Dulin, Anthony Gould and Nick Westbrook-Ikhine.

One AFC West team got stronger while the other slipped further into the abyss. The Broncos added Daiyan Henley to their linebacker group and now own high-end depth behind locked-in starters Alex Singleton and Justin Strnad. Henley, a former third-round pick, was an every-week starter for the Chargers and brings excellent coverage and run defense skills to the vaunted Denver unit as it gears up for a postseason push. The Broncos anticipate receiving a compensatory pick for John Franklin-Myers' departure in free agency, which will help offset the cost of this deal.

It was puzzling to see the Chargers sit on their hands for most of the pre-deadline, considering they are the only winless team in the league, and while the Henley deal finally made them sellers, it was also a surprise for them to strengthen a division rival. They latched onto their other tradeable assets — players like Khalil Mack and even Justin Herbert, who would have commanded far greater returns— while securing only Day 3 capital for Henley.

For the second time in his career, Cousins is a Viking. Minnesota had the most glaring need at quarterback of any team in the league heading into deadline day, and Cousins was the most obvious target given his familiarity with the franchise and offensive system and his status as a bridge for the Raiders to No. 1 pick Fernando Mendoza. He is coming off his worst performance of the year (a three-interception day in a loss to the 49ers) and seemed destined to cede the job to Mendoza in the weeks to come, so the trade makes all kinds of sense for both teams.

Minnesota's two losses this season emphasized its inability to capitalize on owning the league's stingiest defense. It was most apparent on "Monday Night Football" when Carson Wentz struggled mightily in a 23-6 loss to the Bills. Cousins' arrival gives the Vikings a rest-of-season solution and an upgrade over Wentz and Kyler Murray -- one who should be able to force-feed the ball to superstar wideout Justin Jefferson and more accurately distribute it to the secondary options, too.

The Vikings sent their own 2027 third-round pick to the Raiders in the deal and still possess the Eagles' third-rounder, which projects to be a more valuable asset. Las Vegas now owns more Day 2 capital and will put that to use in its construction of a young core around Mendoza.

Following the release of C.J. Gardner-Johnson, the Bills were down to the starting tandem of Cole Bishop and Damar Hamlin at safety. The depth in that group was minimal, and the starting production was that of the fourth-worst pass defense in the NFL. Enter Hooker, who is tied for the league lead with five interceptions. The Titans captain is in the midst of a career year and is allowing a passer rating of just 79.3, which is the best mark of his starting career and the best number since his rookie year when he played off the bench.

The rebuilding Titans now have two second-round picks to go with their two first-rounders in 2027. They are in the throes of a defensive tear-down after they also moved on from Jeffery Simmons and will be among the richest teams in draft capital as they work to stockpile young talent and building blocks for the long-term future.

San Francisco has been dealing with injuries in the receiver room all season. It started during training camp, when Ricky Pearsall went out for the year. It continued in Week 1 with De'Zhaun Stribling injuring his ankle. It got worse in recent weeks with both Mike Evans and Deebo Samuel Sr. experiencing injury issues of their own.

Now, the Niners are adding another veteran to the mix: six-time Pro-Bowler Keenan Allen. He's several years removed from playing at that Pro Bowler level, but he does have 40 catches for 351 yards and a touchdown with the Colts this season.

He should probably be expected to fill snaps at the Z receiver position and in the slot while Evans and Samuel are out. He'll likely rotate in with those guys, Stribling upon his return, and players like Demarcus Robinson and Jordan Watkins. His addition gives the 49ers a reliable option while they deal with injuries and provides solid depth when their main pieces return.

The Cincinnati Bengals were reportedly trading a sixth-round pick to the Eagles for pass rusher Arnold Ebiketie. However, the Bengals front office didn't realize they would also lose a third-round pick in the deal.

Nick Korte of Over The Cap pointed out that Ebiketie was signed by the Eagles as a compensatory free agent. The Bengals lost three compensatory free agents this offseason and signed two. With the addition of Ebiketie, the Bengals would lose a 2027 third-round compensatory pick that was coming after Trey Hendrickson signed with the Ravens.

About an hour later, the trade reportedly collapsed. Korte noted that the Bengals may have thought offensive lineman Cordell Volson counted as one of their CFAs lost. However, he has not played a single snap this year and does not count toward the formula.

Just when it seemed like the Jaguars had filled all of their needs, they went out and made their biggest move of all. Jacksonville's acquisition of Davis is a blockbuster in every sense of the word. The standout defensive tackle will be on the team's books through 2029 after signing a lucrative contract extension with the Eagles earlier this year and will be among the faces of the franchise as it pursues its first Super Bowl. His arrival marks the third trade for a defensive player in one day for Jacksonville and gives them a star in the trenches who should make an already superb group even more impenetrable.

On the other side, Philadelphia is officially in tear-down mode for 2026. Nothing signals a rebuild like moving a foundational player months after locking him into a new contract. The Eagles now have 10 picks in the first two rounds of the next three drafts and can seek an expedited return to playoff contention with a couple of massive draft day hauls.

The Lions should be better off than they were before in the defensive backfield with the addition of Cisco, but by how much? Cisco arrives in a unit that ranks dead last in stopping the pass at 260.9 yards per game. He slipped from his status as an every-week starter and was at the top of the Jets' depth chart for just four of nine games this season. While he remains productive, this is not a home-run transaction for a Detroit team that has to be better on his side of the ball in order to achieve its goals. This could age much better if and when Kerby Joseph returns to the lineup, because Cisco would be a pleasant depth option behind both Joseph and Brian Branch.

New York will have to wait a year to reap the benefits of this trade. It is a success to get something in return for a player who started fewer than half of his games this season, but the Jets need all the draft capital they can stockpile in order to pull themselves out of their rebuild, and 2028 is a long way away.

Jacksonville entered the week with needs at safety and defensive tackle and filled both of them in a matter of hours. Shortly after acquiring Jessie Bates III, the Jaguars made a play for Godchaux, boosting a defense that may already be Super Bowl-caliber. This is a necessary addition for general manager James Gladstone after he watched DaVon Hamilton go down with a torn quad in Week 8 and one that should keep the NFL's No. 5 run defense in the top echelon.

The pick heading to New Orleans will become a 2027 sixth-round pick if the Jaguars acquire one, according to ESPN. They do not currently own one but are projected to receive two compensatory sixth-round picks, according to Spotrac. Even with the caveat on the return, the Saints upgraded in the deal considering they spent a seventh-rounder to acquire Godchaux in a trade last year. He was a questionable fit in a run defense that ranks fifth-worst in the NFL, anyway.

The Jaguars secured a rental for their secondary and an impactful one at that. Bates is a perennial All-Pro safety who not only beefs up the starting lineup with renowned playmaking ability but also adds depth to a group that is dealing with injuries and just lost Eric Murray for the year with an elbow issue. Jacksonville is right in the midst of its contention window and is in play for the AFC's No. 1 seed, so any move that strengthens its Super Bowl chances is justifiable, even if it gives up quite a bit in the way of draft picks to pull it off.

Those picks make this deal a beneficial one for the Falcons, too. Atlanta should have expected to lose Bates in free agency this coming offseason, since he was not among the many players it extended this past summer. The second-round pick is better than any compensatory pick that would have come with his departure, and the 2028 fifth-rounder sweetened the deal even further. Now is the time for the Falcons to turn expiring contracts into future assets, because, at 3-6, they are unlikely to contend for anything beyond a potential first-place finish in the NFL's weakest division.

Baltimore agreed to trade for Cardinals QB Jacoby Brissett and completed the deal over the weekend after its "Thursday Night Football" loss. This was an expected move for the Ravens, because their quarterback situation went from bad to worse in Week 8. The highly anticipated return of starter Lamar Jackson lasted just one play. Jackson suffered a thumb injury and is now on injured reserve. Tyler Huntley, who started in place of Jackson, took over but also went down. He is out for the season with a turf toe injury. The team relied on emergency third quarterback Joe Fagnano to finish the game and started him on Thursday.

Brissett spent the last two seasons with the Cardinals, who benched him in his final game with the team and turned instead to Gardner Minshew. The return of draft capital for a quarterback who lost his job, was unhappy all offseason about his contract situation and projected as a bridge to an eventual first-round pick is a huge success for a rebuilding Arizona team.

In giving up two first-rounders to secure a perennial All-Pro defensive tackle, the Bears went all-in and opened their Super Bowl contention window. The transaction paid immediate dividends when Simmons logged a sack and led the team with six tackles in his Chicago debut: A commanding 34-17 road win over the Seahawks. Mortgaging so much of their future first-round capital showed that the Bears trust their roster, as currently constructed, to compete at the top of the league, and Simmons is the kind of game-changing talent worth the steep price. His production in the pass rush and in run defense could help Chicago maximize its high-octane unit on the other side of the ball led by Caleb Williams.

This move sends the Titans deeper into their rebuild. Questions lie ahead about whether to stick with Cam Ward at quarterback, and with the first-round picks they acquired from dealing Simmons, there will be curiosity as to whether they will use one to land a new signal-caller. Regardless of how they use the picks, moving on from one of the greatest players in franchise history shows how bleak the present is in Tennessee and that all eyes are on the future.

The seemingly nonstop onslaught of injuries hit the 49ers' defensive line, resulting in CJ West landing on injured reserve with a bone chip in his knee and Alfred Collins being ruled out for the year with a torn patellar tendon. That heightened the need for immediate help in the trenches, so the 49ers brought Buckner back after his six-plus years in Indianapolis. The 49ers selected Buckner in the first round of the 2016 draft, and the best years of his prime came during his time in San Francisco, so this is a welcome return for someone the franchise remembers fondly. Buckner remains one of the NFL's top pass-rushing interior defensive linemen and will aid in the 49ers' Super Bowl push.

Buckner's exit marked the second trade in one day for the slumping Colts, who are in the midst of tearing things down with multiple star players on the move and former general manager Chris Ballard out the door. There is an argument to be made that a Day 3 pick was an underwhelming return, but for a 32-year-old veteran on an expiring contract, the Colts were content to get at least something of value for a player who was likely to leave in free agency anyway. Buckner grew publicly frustrated with Indianapolis' struggles, which heightened the anticipation for his trade or an eventual departure at the end of his contract.

Seahawks general manager Jon Schneider is keen to make big-time moves ahead of the trade deadline. This is the fourth consecutive year in which Seattle made a play for an impactful midseason addition to its roster. Nelson is as stellar of an offensive lineman as there is on the trade market this year as a six-time All-Pro and should elevate a struggling Seahawks run game over the long term, even if his arrival did not deliver an immediate boost in the loss to the Bears in his debut. This is also a savvy transaction for the front office given that it stands to receive at least one compensatory third-round pick in 2027; trading a third-rounder for Nelson was thus an easy price to pay.

The Colts got more in return for Nelson than they did for Buckner, which is to be expected considering his status as perhaps the top interior lineman in football. This is still a discount, though, and they remain without a first-round pick in either of the next two drafts. If they can use the third-rounder and next year's fifth-rounder to move up in the order, the trade deadline sale could prove more worthwhile.

Harrison reunited with his college quarterback, C.J. Stroud, upon his arrival in Houston. Will that help him rekindle what has become a disappointing career? His first two games with the Texans were mostly underwhelming, but he did haul in a touchdown reception in Week 7 and could secure a more sizable target share as the team seeks for a go-to No. 2 option behind star pass-catcher Nico Collins. This is trending toward a lost season for the Texans, but they will have another year of control over Harrison and the option to pick up a fifth year in 2028. If they can mold him into the star he was supposed to become out of Ohio State, their passing game will be in excellent shape over the long haul.

Trading Harrison was an admission that he was a bust of a former first-round pick, but to get a second-rounder in the process and to upgrade with a Day 3 pick swap was about as good of a deal as the Cardinals could have landed. Harrison ran his course in the desert and was clearly a poor fit in Mike LaFleur's system, so maximizing his value in the trade was a big win for a team that is not going to contend for anything meaningful in 2026 anyway.

The interior offensive line in Baltimore was a glaring issue last year and into the start of 2026. The Jurgens trade addressed that head-on. Not only did the Ravens find their long-term replacement for Tyler Linderbaum, who left in free agency, but the addition also allowed them to move Olaivavega Ioane from center back to right guard. The full benefits of this move may not show up until Lamar Jackson returns to action, whether that be later in 2026 or next season.

On the Eagles' side, Jurgens' exit effectively marked the end of an era for a once-legendary offensive line that also saw longtime tackle Lane Johnson and legendary position coach Jeff Stoutland enter retirement. It is a turning of the page and one that netted a couple of draft picks for a franchise that now looks destined to rebuild following a six-game losing streak. Philadelphia had been playing Jurgens out of position, anyway.

If Porter cannot fix the Cowboys' defense, then that unit might be broken beyond repair in 2026. Dallas made a splash to address its greatest weakness and has thus far seen the exact kind of individual impact it envisioned from the star cornerback, as he allowed a diminutive 39.6 passer rating and just three completions on 11 targets across his first four games with the team. Still, the unit around him is in shambles. And with Porter set to reach unrestricted free agency this offseason, this could be a one-year rental that fails to produce a postseason appearance.

The Steelers made it clear that they were not going to shell out market-resetting money to keep Porter around on a long-term contract extension. By declining to meet his demands as he sat out the start of the season, they only had one viable option, which was to trade him. A first-round pick would have been the preferable return package, but Pittsburgh received the next-best thing with both a second-rounder and 2027 draft capital. That sixth-round pick in next year's draft already vested, as it was conditional upon Porter being active for just two games with the Cowboys.

The torn knee ligaments Jaxson Dart sustained in Week 2 made the Giants desperate for quarterback help, because the rest of the roster remained talented enough to compete for the NFC East crown. The McCarthy acquisition at first came across as puzzling given his own struggles to stay healthy, his underwhelming play when available and the fact that New York already has a franchise quarterback and thus has little to gain from McCarthy potentially developing into a viable starter. But it has aged better than expected. McCarthy threw for 243 yards and a touchdown in the fourth quarter in Week 6, earned the starting job over Jameis Winston and promptly delivered a road win over the Texans to place the Giants in command of the division.

Getting anything in return for McCarthy after a disastrous start to his career was a win for the Vikings. He slipped all the way down to the third line of Minnesota's depth chart behind a couple of low-ceiling veterans in Kyler Murray and Carson Wentz and was clearly no longer part of the franchise's plans.

Why Lincoln Educational Services Stock Soared Again Today

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Yet another peer just posted an estimates-beating quarter.

For the second trading session in a row, Lincoln Educational Services (LINC +4.54%) stock posted a solid gain on Tuesday. The company's shares continued to benefit from bullish momentum sparked by its impressive third-quarter earnings report and similarly encouraging results from a peer. Its shares closed Tuesday more than 4.5% higher.

That peer is American Public Education (APEI +2.84%). It published its third-quarter results after market close on Monday, and its stock rose the following day. That was mainly thanks to enrollment increases in both of its core operating divisions and to management's top- and bottom-line guidance revisions.

This will sound familiar to Lincoln Educational shareholders, as these dynamics were at play in their company's third quarter. What also helped Lincoln was its double-digit increases in student starts, population, and overall revenue -- and, yes, a guidance raise.

This, along with the recent quarterlies of other education stocks, suggests that demand is rising for the vocational and specialty learning services these companies provide.

To me, two key factors are contributing to that demand. The first is the shortage of skilled trade and technical workers in the U.S. This has been caused, in large part, by several generations that have placed a premium on "traditional" college and university degree programs over vocational learning. The second is the ever-rising cost of such programs, which has made them harder to afford for many Americans, particularly amid rising consumer prices.

These feel like durable mid- to long-term trends, as I don't see the causes behind them melting away anytime soon. I think the education sector will continue to post encouraging growth, and I'd consider its better stocks to be buy candidates -- Lincoln Educational included.

6 R-Rated Superhero Shows Better Than The Boys

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The superhero landscape looked very different back in 2019 before The Boys came around, as despite there being a fair few R-rated projects on the market, none were as crude or graphic as Prime Video's mega hit. Alongside putting a completely different spin on the genre by having mainly evil superheroes, The Boys also parodied Marvel, DC, and various other franchises.

However, with The Boys ending earlier in 2026, it left a big gap in the world of R-rated superhero shows, and while spinoffs such as Vought Rising and The Boys: Mexico will continue this universe, things simply won't be the same without the main series. Fortunately, there is already an abundance of great competitors out there.

Originally, it looked like Gen V would be The Boys' replacement before being canceled, but if you are looking for R-rated hits that are even better than anything this violent franchise produced, these six projects are definitely worth checking out.

Despite lasting just two seasons, The Punisher feels like a complete series that extends Frank Castle's story after first appearing in Daredevil. Getting to learn about the tragic events that turned him from a loving husband and father to a merciless killing machine is fascinating, and The Punisher certainly doesn't pull any punches with its violence.

As we've seen in the 2004 movie, characterizing Frank isn't always easy, as without the right nuance, he can feel empty and hard to connect with. However, Jon Bernthal delivers an outstanding performance that shows the humanity behind this vigilante, making it easier to root for him, despite not always agreeing with his actions.

The first season comes across as more personal, with Frank getting vengeance on those who were responsible for the death of his family, and the supporting cast is a key part of his journey. Ebon Moss-Bachrach, Amber Rose Revah, and Ben Barnes all do a great job with their respective characters, and while it can be an uncomfortable watch at times, The Punisher's story is ultimately very satisfying.

Season 2 presents a different vibe, with Giorgia Whigham's Amy Bendix bringing out the more fatherly side of Frank. Not only does he risk his own life to protect Amy, but he stands up for injustice outside his own personal grievances, with the final scene showing him fully embracing his Punisher persona.

Considering the character has been brought back as part of the MCU and even had his own special, it's clear Bernthal made a big impact as the Punisher, and the show successfully delivers exactly the type of brutal action needed to make it an all-time great that eclipses The Boys.

With James Gunn and Peter Safran stepping down from their leadership positions at DC Studios, the future of Lanterns is uncertain, but after just one season, it has already made a huge impression. Culturally, the show was massive, as it rejuvenated interest in the Green Lantern mythos and had people constantly bringing new theories and memes every week.

Sure, it definitely proved divisive as comic readers had major issues with the character and story direction, but as a whole, Lanterns was incredibly popular. The season 1 finale pulled in over 10 million viewers in just three days, and even those who hate the show can't deny how exceptional Kyle Chandler is as Hal Jordan, with Aaron Pierre shining opposite him.

Even though the series leans more into the mystery and detective genre than classic superhero elements, there are still several fun action sequences that definitely qualify it as a superhero project over something like The Penguin. Additionally, Lanterns sets up John Stewart to be more powerful in Man of Tomorrow, which should make up for the lack of major fight scenes.

It's rare that a modern show makes such a seismic impact in just nine episodes, but in terms of quality and consistency, Lanterns trumps The Boys, despite the latter having higher highs. The desire for a second season and Hal's return highlights how good the HBO series really is, and hopefully there's still a future for this surprise hit.

Much like Lanterns, Peacemaker's future is uncertain, even more so since James Gunn directed it, but it seems like season 2 was always planned to be the last anyway. Still, in just 16 episodes, Peacemaker manages to turn a bunch of obscure and original characters into fan favorites who audiences became 100% invested in.

John Cena's Peacemaker first appeared in 2021's The Suicide Squad, and while he was originally portrayed as arrogant and overconfident, viewers came around to him until he betrayed Rick Flag. That made centering a show around him extremely risky, yet Cena delivers a career-high performance full of emotion and vulnerability, adding so much depth to this anti-hero.

With an absolutely phenomenal cast for support, the series really thrives in its character work, making it almost essential that they return in future DCU projects. Both seasons feel incredibly different as they take place under completely different hierarchies, yet Gunn's vision allowed them to connect and achieve a similar quality.

Once again, it's a show that had people talking every week, predicting what would happen next, and somehow, people managed to guess season 2's most outlandish twist. At its core, Peacemaker is fun, hilarious, brutal, and surprisingly deep, cementing it as one of the greatest live-action superhero shows of the 21st century.

Its killer soundtrack only gives it more personality, and it has two of the best superhero show opening credits ever made, showing how much care was put into crafting this legendary series.

After the highly underrated 2009 movie, it wasn't clear how much legs the Watchmen franchise had in the highly competitive superhero market, but the TV show that premiered a decade later didn't disappoint. The series follows mostly different characters than the film, creating a unique spectacle, but one that is every bit entertaining.

Even though it's an original story, the tone and visuals capture what made the comics so great, and it isn't afraid to dive into culturally relevant topics. It tackles issues like racism in a mature manner, while having a compelling direction that isn't easy to predict, keeping viewers on their toes throughout all nine episodes.

Like the other entries on this list, the acting is top drawer, with Jeremy Irons standing out as the unhinged Ozymandias. The score is also worth mentioning, since it helps create the moody tone that makes Watchmen so gritty. That said, the show isn't afraid to get a little goofy at times either, with the humor separating it from DC's usual brand of comedy.

The story ties together nicely, and while the ending is packed, Watchmen still sticks the landing, making it no surprise it's so widely celebrated. It won 11 Emmys and was nominated for 26 in total, emphasizing the quality of this limited series, which didn't overstay its welcome.

Over 10 years ago, Daredevil debuted on Netflix, and to this day, many still see it as the greatest superhero show ever made. As a blind lawyer by day and a ruthless vigilante at night, Matt Murdock is the perfect protagonist for a dark, bloody show, and the three-season masterpiece couldn't have portrayed him any better.

Matt possesses all the charm and charisma needed to make him instantly likable, yet his disability and tragic origins cement him as a true underdog. Luckily, Foggy Nelson and Karen Page act as the optimal allies for him, which makes it all the more heartbreaking when he pushes them away, even if it's supposed to be for selfless reasons.

The drama is ever-present, and the choreography is perhaps still the gold standard for superhero projects, with that iconic one-shot hallway brawl remaining relevant all these years later. That's not to overlook Frank Castle's prison fight or the office brawl between Daredevil and Bullseye in season 3.

Speaking of Bullseye, he and Kingpin were so good that they both returned for Daredevil: Born Again with the same actors, confirming these villains were irreplaceable. Daredevil also has fantastic cinematography, with vibrant colors and shots helping forge an identity that the Disney+ show couldn't replicate, despite trying its best.

Between the fantastic cast, gripping storylines, flawless editing, and all the other qualities Daredevil possesses, it's no surprise it has such a stellar reputation. It is a superhero show that changed the genre forever, and while The Boys did the same, it simply doesn't measure up to Netflix's Daredevil.

While The Boys deserves a lot of credit for breaking the traditional Marvel and DC dominance by becoming one of the biggest superhero shows of the past decade, its Prime Video competitor did the same and is even better. Invincible arrived two years after The Boys, but the animated hit is far more consistent, and still has a healthy future ahead of it.

As a faithful comic adaptation, Invincible retells the story of Robert Kirkman's original series, albeit with some tweaks and additions. Its vast roster of characters is among its biggest strengths, as alongside having a fleshed-out, complex protagonist, there are so many wonderful heroes, villains, and other supporting allies that make the universe feel alive.

There is no holding back when it comes to fights either, as the gory injuries make even The Boys look tame at times. Invincible also does a great job of adding genuinely hilarious gags and jokes into its otherwise serious narrative, which is far less shocking or in your face than its Prime Video counterpart.

Some of its very best episodes have near-perfect ratings on IMDb, putting them among the greatest in TV history, and mixed with a fantastic soundtrack, A-list cast, and constant drama, there is no denying that Invincible is a better superhero series than The Boys.

The fact that the show managed to overcome its biggest weakness by shortening the gap between seasons to release regularly means it is bound to keep getting better, and with several seasons still to come for Invincible, it deserves to be recognized as one of the best in the genre.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Stock Market Today, Nov. 10: Wall Street's Rally Hits a Roadblock as Hot Inflation Raises the Stakes for the Fed

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Yesterday's rebound lost momentum as rising Treasury yields and renewed inflation concerns weighed on stocks

Wall Street's rebound stalled on Tuesday, November 10, as hotter-than-expected inflation data reignited concerns about Federal Reserve interest rate policy and sent Treasury yields higher. The Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC -0.74%) dropped 0.74% to 25,066, retreating from Monday's gains, while the S&P 500 (^GSPC -0.38%) slipped 0.38% to 7,521 and the Dow Jones Industrial Average (^DJI -0.30%) fell 0.30% to 52,232.

Selling pressure extended beyond equities as gold fell 1.84% to $4,038.70, while the benchmark 10-year Treasury yield climbed to 4.88%, approaching the psychologically important 5% threshold. Market performance was mixed across sectors, with consumer cyclical stocks leading gains and industrials advancing, while healthcare lagged. The divergence highlighted an increasingly selective market as investors reassessed the outlook for interest rates and economic growth.

Despite the broader market decline, several prominent stocks posted strong gains on Tuesday. Meta Platforms (META +0.00%) surged more than 5% after JPMorgan (JPM +0.00%) added the social media giant to its Focus List, while Sea Limited (SE -1.49%) jumped 8.1% following stronger-than-expected quarterly revenue.

On the downside, Nvidia (NVDA -5.47%) plunged 5.52% amid an ongoing investigation involving CEO Jensen Huang, adding to uncertainty ahead of the chipmaker's upcoming earnings report.

Tuesday's sell-off underscores a growing challenge for equity investors heading into year-end. With inflation proving more persistent than expected, markets are increasingly pricing in another Federal Reserve rate hike in December. Higher borrowing costs could weigh on corporate profitability and put additional pressure on stock valuations, particularly among growth companies whose valuations depend heavily on future earnings.

However, the divergence in individual stock performance suggests investors are becoming more selective rather than abandoning equities altogether. Strong corporate earnings and favorable company-specific developments continue to attract capital, even as broader market conditions deteriorate. This could favor businesses with healthy balance sheets, strong cash flow, and the ability to sustain growth without relying heavily on external financing.

Looking ahead, investors should watch upcoming inflation data, Federal Reserve commentary, and Treasury yields for signs of whether monetary conditions will tighten further. With the 10-year yield approaching 5%, the sustainability of Wall Street's recent rebound may depend increasingly on corporate earnings delivering enough growth to justify elevated valuations.

DC Studios Exit: James Gunn and Peter Safran Spent Big, Lost Money and Didn’t Hook Audiences

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

After “Superman” landed in theaters last summer, former Warner Bros. Discovery CEO David Zaslav was ready to pop Champagne. Although the movie had only been playing on the big screen for three days, he touted the “bold 10-year plan” of DC Studios co-chiefs James Gunn and Peter Safran and promised “the DC vision is clear” and “the momentum is real.”

Fast forward a year and a half, and those plans have imploded. Gunn and Safran were dismissed from the top of the comic book empire, with Marvel Studios alum Jeremy Latcham taking over as president and chief creative officer of DC Studios. Related Stories Korea Box Office: Anime Hit 'Chiikawa' Retains Lead as Brad Pitt Thriller 'Heart of the Beast' Debuts Korea Box Office: Anime Phenomenon 'Chiikawa' Staves Off Corporate Thriller 'Final Interview' to Retain Top Spot

The duo’s ouster wasn’t a total shock, even though they had been assured they were part of David Ellison’s business leadership team at Skydance after Paramount’s $111 billion acquisition of WBD closed in October. Had the two stayed, the org change would have represented a demotion. Under the previous regime, they answered directly to Zaslav and not movie co-chairs Michael De Luca and Pamela Abdy. However, Ellison and co-CEO Ynon Kreiz made plans to shift Gunn and Safran’s reporting structure into new Skydance Motion Picture Group chiefs Dana Goldberg and Josh Greenstein.

The executive shuffle is yet another setback for DC Studios, which has struggled for years to compete with Disney’s Marvel Cinematic Universe. Their short-lived leadership stint at DC follows multiple turnovers over the past decade, starting with comic book veterans Geoff Johns and Jon Berg and then Walter Hamada. After DC’s last iteration of superhero movies flamed out with duds like “The Flash” and “Aquaman and the Lost Kingdom,” Gunn and Safran were hired in 2022 to overhaul some of the company’s most lucrative IP. Efforts to right the ship were mixed at best — with tentpoles and television shows that didn’t always generate enough excitement to justify their price tags. Sources suggest the thinking was they spent big — and lost too much — on projects that just weren’t resonating with audiences.

Gunn’s “Superman,” starring David Corenswet — the duo’s first big swing as bosses — was touted as a hit with $618 million against a $225 million budget. Those ticket sales were promising enough, given the general decline in popularity of comic book movies. But they didn’t necessarily signal the kind of lasting enthusiasm that spawns an interconnected universe. Momentum all but evaporated with July’s money-loser “Supergirl,” which flopped with $126 million globally against a $170 million budget. And while October’s “Clayface” performed modestly with $109 million against a $44 million budget, sources suggest the body-horror thriller won’t break even until the studio factors in downstream revenues.

Insiders have raised eyebrows at Gunn and Safran’s decision to focus on lesser-known characters, like Superman’s cousin Kara El-Zor, aka Supergirl, and the Batman villain Clayface, rather than building out the franchise with marquee names. Although superhero movies have been hit or miss, the ones that spotlight notable characters can still pack a box-office punch. July’s “Spider-Man: Brand New Day” became the third-biggest movie in history with $2.5 billion, and December’s “Avengers: Endgame,” which brings back just about all of Earth’s Mightiest Heroes, could challenge the web-slinging Marvel sequel’s box office records.

“The industry has taken away the wrong lessons from Marvel’s success,” a studio insider tells Variety. “Higher budgets don’t equal quality storytelling.”

Television efforts, too, were uneven. DC’s main small-screen success was “The Penguin,” a “Batman” spinoff led by Colin Farrell, was greenlit before Gunn and Safran took over. But the rest of the slate has generated less fanfare. Gunn’s “Peacemaker” series, anchored by John Cena, debuted in 2022 before he stepped into the studio job. Although initial season was popular, the second season – which launched three years later – failed to match the buzz of the first. “Lanterns,” their first live-action TV show, had posted decent viewership. Yet an insider at the company suggests the crime drama isn’t quite capturing the zeitgeist, pointing out the show didn’t get renewed for a second season.

“People cannot wait to make another season of a hit show,” the source said.

Safran, a steward of New Line’s wildly profitable “Conjuring” universe and producer on “Shazam!” and “Aquaman,” was used to cutting checks and poring over balance sheets before he was hired at DC. However Gunn, best known for directing Marvel’s “Guardians of the Galaxy” trilogy, was always a curious choice for the job. Gunn had a tricky balancing act, needing to shepherd his own directorial efforts while co-managing a slate. Collaborators praised Gunn’s ability to work quickly and efficiently on simultaneous projects. But even he admitted the challenge of needing to multitask and handle the “corporate” nature of the gig.

“The thing my ADHD hyperfocus has had the most difficulty with has been dividing my time between projects I’m incredibly passionate about and the ones I’m not so much and all the things I have to do as a studio head,” Gunn, who regularly converses with comic book enthusiasts on social media, wrote on X after his ouster.

The pair will remain involved with DC, though with less authority. Gunn is directing next summer’s “Superman: Man of Tomorrow,” and the two are producing the 2027 television series “The People v. Gorilla Grodd,” with Skyler Gisondo as his “Superman” character Jimmy Olsen and Jimmy Tatro as Gorilla Grodd, and another season of “Creature Commandos.”

It’s unclear what will become of Gunn and Safran’s decade-long creative strategy of sequels, spinoffs and stand-alone stories. After “Superman: Man of Tomorrow,” which returns Corenswet’s Man of Steel and Nicholas Hoult’s supervillain Lex Luther to the forefront, the only other movie in the pipeline is “The Batman Part II.” After several delays, the sequel is scheduled to be released in 2028 — six years after Robert Pattinson’s original Caped Crusader adventure. Their oft-teased “Wonder Woman” reboot never got off the ground. And though Gunn has hinted he’s writing another DC-related project, it’ll be up to Latcham to decide whether to continue with the vision of the prior regime.

Internally, there’s hope that Latcham — an executive who helped shape the MCU — will get back to basics with DC.

“Jeremy is the kind of guy who would go to Comic-Con to get an autograph from someone,” says another source, “not someone who wants to be there signing autographs.”

Kia launches two new PV5 electric van variants in the UK

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Kia opened orders for two new PV5 electric van variants, the PV5 Chassis and PV5 Cargo L2/H2, in the UK.

The PV5 is Kia’s first fully electric van from its Platform Beyond Vehicle (PBV) business. Built on Hyundai Motor’s dedicated modular skateboard platform, E-GMP.S, Kia can easily swap body styles to enable “limitless possibilities.”

So far, the PV5 has mostly been available in Passenger and Cargo body styles with several different variations.

Kia added the PV5 Chassis, an open-bed model that can be converted into just about anything. In the UK, Kia is offering conversions through TGS Automotive Group, including lightweight tipper, Dropside, and Box solutions.

The PV5 Chassis is available exclusively with the long-range 71.2 kWh battery in the UK, which Kia said could offer a potential WLTP range of up to 258 miles (416 km).

For reference, the PV5 Cargo L2/H1, which uses the same 71.2 kWh battery, offers a WLTP range of up to 258 miles (416 km). Other markets will also have a standard-range 51.1 kWh option.

The Chassis model is 4,610 mm long, 1,895 mm wide, and 1,935 mm tall, with a 2,995 mm wheelbase.

Its compact size and 5.5-meter turning radius, Kia said, make it easy to maneuver in city streets, construction sites, and other tight work areas.

Inside, the PV5 Chassis features two seats. Like other variants, it includes a 12.9″ navigation screen and a 7.5″ driver display with standard wireless Apple CarPlay and Android Auto.

The Chassis model supports DC fast charging up to 120 kW, enabling the battery to charge from 10% to 80% in about 30 minutes. Using an 11 kW AC supply, it takes about 6 hours and 30 minutes to charge from 10% to 100%.

Payload depends on the configuration. Kia said the Dropside offers a payload of up to 633 kg, while the Tipper can carry 561 kg.

In the UK, the PV5 Chassis is now on sale from £32,916 ($43,400) on the road, including the expected £5,000 ($6,700) UK Zero Emission Van Grant.

However, it’s an “incomplete vehicle,” as Kia describes it. The converted PV5 Dropside is priced from £40,110 ($53,500) on the road, including the ZEVG, while the PV5 Tipper starts at £41,310 ($55,000).

Kia also launched the larger PV5 Cargo L2/H2 in the UK. The new variant is 4,695mm long, 1,895mm wide, and 2,200mm high, with a 2,995mm wheelbase.

The taller roof opens up an internal cargo-area height of 1,815mm. It also offers a greater load capacity than the L1/H1 and L2/H2, with 5.2 cubic meters.

It’s available in three- and four-door configurations with the choice of two or three seats. The three-door versions have a maximum payload of 630 kg, while the four-door models have dual sliding doors and offer a payload of up to 600 kg. Both versions can tow up to 1,500 kg.

Like the Chassis model, the PV5 Cargo L2/H2 is available in the UK exclusively with the 71.2 kWh battery, which provides a WLTP range of up to 239 miles.

Inside, it features the same setup as the other PV5 variants, including a 12.9″ touchscreen navigation screen and a 7.5″ driver display.

The base PV5 Cargo L2/H2 Essential two-seat starts at £35,016 ($46,700), on the road, including ZEVG.

Grading the trade: Josh Downs gives Chiefs WR help they needed, but is he the right fit?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Just prior to the trade deadline, the Kansas City Chiefs acquired wide receiver Josh Downs from the Indianapolis Colts. The Chiefs will receive Downs and a 2027 seventh-round pick, and the Colts will get back fourth- and fifth-round picks in 2027.

Downs, 25, who recently requested a trade, got his wish and now will team up with Patrick Mahomes. In nine games (seven starts) with the Colts this season, Downs caught 33 passes for 354 yards and one TD on 57 targets.

Downs also had a team-high seven catches for 72 yards on nine targets in the Colts' 33-30 loss in Kansas City back in Week 2.

Who won this trade? Let's dive into the debate.

The Chiefs were in big need of adding receiver help. Not one wideout on the roster has either 50 targets or 300 yards receiving on the season. Downs, sharing the lead role in Indy along with Keenan Allen (and with Alec Pierce hurt), has surpassed both marks.

That's not to say that he instantly will become Patrick Mahomes' go-to target. But it's hard not to imagine Downs stepping in and earning a fairly sizable role in Kansas City's offense, assuming he can develop a relationship with Mahomes and pick up the playbook quickly.

Downs has primarily been a slot receiver in his career, although his work rate playing outside has increased slightly in 2026 out of need. It would make sense to put him in the slot, although what makes him, Rashee Rice and Xavier Worthy attractive is that they can line up in multiple spots.

Rice's numbers over the past year-plus have been slightly better inside, but Worthy has been far more efficient as a perimeter receiver in his career. That likely means Downs can take those snaps and do what he does best: run efficient routes, separate quickly and generate first downs. The biggest drawback is that Downs is not a deep threat, which was probably their biggest need.

This move also can help the Chiefs' punt-return situation. Nikko Remigio has struggled in that role this season. Downs has only returned two punts this season, but he has a very respectable 9.8-yard career average on punt returns.

The Chiefs know that Downs is a free agent after the season, and they had to be comfortable with the idea of this trade potentially being a half-season rental. Thinning out their Day 3 pick allotment stings a little, but the price wasn't that steep in the big picture, especially when you compare it to what the Seahawks paid for Rashid Shaheed (fourth- and fifth-round picks) and what the Jaguars dealt for Jakobi Meyers (fourth- and sixth-rounders) before last year's deadline.

This move is about taking advantage of a championship window with Mahomes showing he's recovered from his knee injury and Travis Kelce still performing at a relatively high level in his expected swan-song season.

Downs requested a trade and got his wish. The Colts likely weren't convinced they had to extend him, and Downs probably wasn't returning to Indianapolis next season anyway, so trading him now made perfect sense instead of waiting for compensatory-pick returns more than a year from now.

It fits right in line with the other trades they've made since firing GM Chris Ballard, shipping out one-time franchise pillars Quenton Nelson and DeForest Buckner and also dealing Allen. When you consider what they got back for Downs, which was two early Day 3 picks, it was a pretty fair market price.

Not having a first-rounder next spring from the Sauce Gardner will continue to sting. But with the trades, they've managed to replenish the cupboard a bit. They already have seven Day 3 picks to work with and are projected to receive two more compensatory selections in that same range.

Having those picks can allow the Colts potentially to slide up in the draft, and quarterback could be on the menu given the uncertainty surrounding Daniel Jones, even if Jones was not traded by the deadline.

They'll also eventually need to replenish a gutted WR room, but for now the Colts can see what they have in the likes of Nick Westbrook-Ikhine, Anthony Gould, Ashton Dulin, Deion Burks, Laquon Treadwell and, of course, Pierce, when he's back healthy again. It wouldn't be stunning if the Colts only bring back a few of these players next year, but the remainder of the season will help them decide who stays and who goes.

'Many were skeptical': Dejan Kulusevski reveals Tottenham staff doubts in shock injury admission

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Your weekly briefing on Tottenham, featuring team news and expert analysis from Sam Tabuteau

I would like to be emailed about offers, event and updates from Evening Standard. Read our privacy notice.

Dejan Kulusevski has revealed that some Tottenham staff doubted that he would be able to recover from the knee injury that kept him out for 18 months.

The Swede made his long-anticipated comeback to action in Spurs’ 1-0 win over Coventry last month after a lengthy lay-off that had him wondering whether he would ever be able to kick a ball professionally again.

The time on the sidelines forced the forward to go as far as telling his mother that he thought his career was over at the mere age of 26.

And as Kulusevski began losing faith in his recovery process, he has now revealed that some in north London felt the same way.

“There were many who didn’t believe it, many were skeptical. Even at the club,” he told Fotbollskanalen.

“I’ve been disappointed in people during this period, but at the same time I have to forgive everything.

“Sometimes you wish you’d received more help and so on. But at the same time, I understand we’re all part of a business.

“I’ve moved on and I love being at the club. I received a really good welcome when I came back.”

Kulusevski has returned to international duty this week after missing the World Cup last summer.

He has confirmed that while he is able to complete a full 90 minutes in both Nations League games against Bosnia and Herzegovina and Poland, that will not be the case when he runs out for Graham Potter’s side.

“I’ll get to play some minutes in both games,” he continued.

“Around 30 minutes in each. That’s what’s been said. It’s better than zero, so I’m happy.”

“I actually can play 90 minutes in both matches. But that’s what I can do. I’m not the one who decides.”

Gochman Collection Builds a Home for Native Art in Westchester

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The private collection opened its Branchline location in Katonah as a way to bring Native art into public view, though questions linger about its engagement with the local Lenape community.

KATONAH, NY — In 1680, English colonists bought the land that would become Bedford, New York, from a Munsee Lenape sachem named Katonah, meaning “great hill.” Today, his name belongs to a Westchester village that is now home to Branchline, the Gochman Family Collection’s (GFC) new gallery and studio space, which opened to the public on November 6 with Line Break, an exhibition of works drawn primarily from its holdings of contemporary Native American art.

Since its founding in 2021, GFC has collected over 800 works with support from Native and non-Native gallerists, artists, and curators alike, including Creative Director and Curator Rachel Martin (Tlingít), curator and Hyperallergic contributor Shándíín Brown (Diné), and Director Laura Phipps, who also serves as Branchline’s director. Designed by Elizabeth Roberts Architects, the new space occupies two historic storefronts steps from Katonah’s Metro-North station, with free-admission galleries, skylit artist studios, a community ceramics studio, and apartments for invited artists.

Branchline makes a public home for what has essentially been a lending operation: According to GFC, more than 260 of its works have appeared in over 100 exhibitions at institutions including the Metropolitan Museum of Art, the Whitney Museum of American Art, and The Broad, and it charges no loan fees. That model speaks to a known problem with private collecting, explained Joseph Pierce (Cherokee), professor and founding director of the Native American and Indigenous Studies Initiative at Stony Brook University. “Just because you collect something doesn’t mean you’re going to show it,” Pierce told Hyperallergic. “The risk is that it doesn’t circulate.”

Create a free account to read more articles each month, and get the best of Hyperallergic in your inbox.

This residency at the U-M Stamps School, in partnership with the U-M Museum of Art (UMMA), offers artists a $20,000 honorarium, housing, studio space, and a culminating exhibition in UMMA’s prominent Stenn Gallery.

The Bayeux tapestry, a new biography of Edward Hopper, The Met's exhibition catalogue on Krasner and Pollock, what architecture is, and more

Funding for tuition, healthcare, and stipends is available. Apply to the University of Maryland, Baltimore County program by Feb 1 to be considered for financial support.

Generously funded, renowned faculty and visiting artists, spacious studios, and an exhibition at the Manetti Shrem Museum of Art.

Veteran Talent Agent Sasha Passero Leaving IAG

Sacha Passero is departing IAG after almost 12 years at the talent agency and its predecessor APA. Passero’s contract has come to an end and was not renewed.

A talent agent based in New York, Passero joined APA from Kazarian/Spencer/Ruskin & Associates at the beginning of 2015. The roster of clients she has worked with the agency includes Lily Gladstone, Alicia Witt, Lyndsy Fonseca, Arielle Kebble, Mariana Treviño, Tom Payne and Clark Backo.

Passero started her career as an assistant at Innovative Artists before joining KSR in 2011 where she became an agent.

End of year is when the bulk of contracts are up, with agencies and management companies trimming ranks by not re-upping everyone. MLB rumors: Mets to meet with Tarik Skubal, Rangers' Corey Seager plan, plus Blue Jays, Cubs, Red Sox, more

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

This is an important week on the baseball calendar. First and foremost, it is awards week. Kevin McGonigle and Sal Stewart were named the Rookies of the Year on Monday. The Managers of the Year will be announced Tuesday, Cy Youngs and Relievers of the Year will be announced Wednesday, and finally the MVPs will be announced Thursday.

Also, the GM Meetings are this week in Las Vegas. The GM Meetings are not the Winter Meetings in terms of the sheer volume of rumors and transactions, but they do usually provide some news. There could be more than usual this year given the near certainty of a lockout on Dec. 1, which would also mean there are no Winter Meetings. This may be the only time everyone is in the same room this winter. The three weeks between now and Dec. 1 could be quite active.

We've already ranked the top 50 free agents and the top 25 trade candidates.

With that in mind, here are Tuesday's hot stove rumors.

The Mets have a meeting scheduled with two-time Cy Young winner Tarik Skubal, the clear top free agent avaiable this offseason, later this week, reports the New York Post. Skubal will meet with teams in California, presumably at the offices of agent Scott Boras. Owner Steve Cohen is expected to push for Skubal, according to SNY; president of baseball operations David Stearns has long been reluctant to sign big-money, long-term deals for pitchers, though pitchers of Skubal's caliber are rarely available. The Mets are also seeking first base help and bullpen reinforcements.

Nolan McLean and Christian Scott are the only locks for New York's 2027 rotation at the moment. Justin Hagenman, Sean Manaea, Kodai Senga, Zac Thornton, and Jonah Tong are next in line to make starts. Tylor Megill is expected back from Tommy John surgery early next year as well. The Mets have some depth but clearly need more impact.

The Rangers signed Corey Seager to a 10-year, $325 million deal prior to the 2022 season and it paid off quite handsomely with a World Series title in 2023 (Seager was the World Series MVP). With five years and roughly $155 million left on the deal, however, the Rangers appear ready to trade him.

The Rangers "intend to explore moving Seager at the general managers' meetings," reports The Athletic.

Seager, 32, was stellar from 2023 to 2025 in spite of a litany of injuries, averaging 6.2 WAR per season with a .294 average and 158 OPS+. He actually averaged 8.7 WAR per 162 games. Only the injuries kept him from making multiple serious MVP runs. Last season, though, was a step back. In 102 games, he hit .227/.312/.424 with 20 home runs, 51 RBI and 3.1 WAR.

The injury history keeps getting more extensive, too, as he's dealt with injuries to his thumb, hamstring, hip, other hamstring and back in the last four years.

With a lackluster, at best, shortstop class in free agency, now might be the perfect time for the Rangers to move on from Seager, assuming a deal can be worked out with the years and dollars left on his contract. Texas also has Sebastian Walcott, one of the top prospects in baseball, ready in the wings.

The Blue Jays are among the teams with interest in free agent second baseman Brandon Lowe, according to Sportsnet. Ernie Clement, Toronto's primary second baseman in 2026, crashed hard in the second half and has always fit better as a super utility man who plays regularly at different positions. Josh Smith is the team's other second base option.

Lowe would bring needed power to a team that finished dead last in home runs this past season and had just one player, Kazuma Okamoto (33), clear 15 home runs. Only four Blue Jays hit even 10 homers. Also, Toronto's lineup is mostly right-handed. Lowe would add power and also a lefty bat to provide balance. We ranked him as the 13th-best free agent available.

Adding rotation help is a "top priority," White Sox GM Chris Getz said during an MLB Network Radio interview on Tuesday. "We're going to exhaust the starter market, both free agency -- we'll take a look -- along with trades. That is our top priority," Getz said. He also confirmed that Grant Taylor, the team's postseason closer, will move into the rotation. Hagen Smith will remain in the bullpen.

The White Sox have solid enough rotation depth with Taylor joining Sean Burke, Luis Castillo, Anthony Kay, Davis Martin, David Sandlin, Noah Schultz, and perhaps Shane Smith and Drew Thorpe. There is room for improvement, though, particularly at the top. Chicago could use a bona fide ace, not that one is easy to acquire or abundantly available.

Add the Padres to the list of teams that have shown interest in Skubal, reports MLB Network. San Diego has a major need in the rotation -- Joe Musgrove, who hasn't made a start since the 2024 postseason, and Randy Vásquez are their only rotation locks -- and a new ownership group that seems willing to spend in its first offseason.

Skubal is far and away the offseason's best free agent. He is also a Scott Boras client, and, historically, top Boras clients take the largest contract offer regardless of team situation. That isn't to say the Padres are a bad situation. Not at all. They're a very good team that is in the postseason most years, plus they play in San Diego. Who doesn't love San Diego?

The Padres are also connected to several Japanese players, according to The Athletic. That includes MLB free agents Seiya Suzuki and Shota Imanaga, as well as players coming over from Nippon Professional Baseball, like third baseman Teruaki Sato.

Free agent lefty Adrian Morejon is drawing interest as a starting pitcher, but he prefers to remain in the bullpen, according to The Athletic. Even during the season, there were rumblings that Morejon would like to be given a chance to start in 2027. He came up through the minors as a starter and only remained in the bullpen because he was so dominant in a late-inning role.

We ranked Morejon as the offseason's second-best free agent on the idea that he would move into the rotation. The belief within the game is that he has a chance to be an excellent starter, not just a back-end piece. Either way, Morejon is a very good pitcher, and at 28, he's also one of the youngest free agents available. He should have several peak years remaining.

Although they are disinclined to pursue Tarik Skubal, the Cubs hope to add three established starting pitchers this offseason, reports The Athletic. Of the nine pitchers to make at least nine starts for Chicago in 2027, seven are free agents. Only Ben Brown, who recently had neck surgery (per ESPN), and Edward Cabrera, who had a 5.96 ERA and was demoted to the bullpen, remain.

Left-hander Justin Steele is expected back from UCL surgery next year, though he suffered a setback this summer and wasn't able to return as hoped in September. Others like Javier Assad and Jordan Wicks are depth options more than full-time rotation pieces. This free agent class offers plenty of second- and third-tier starters. That's where the Cubs are expected to shop.

Unlike last offseason, the Mets are not interested in a major overhaul despite their disappointing year, reports MLB.com. The Mets went 74-88 and finished in last place in 2026. The only silver lining to the disappointing season was the ability to give a long runway to rookies like Carson Benge and Nolan McLean, both of whom finished in the top four of the Rookie of the Year voting.

The Mets went 83-79 in 2025 and missed the postseason on the final day of the season. They made significant changes last winter, most notably jettisoning stalwarts Pete Alonso and Brandon Nimmo, then had a worse season in 2026. In September, president of baseball operations David Stearns admitted that much turnover created an "uncapped downside scenario," which is executive-speak for "there was too much risk."

Not surprisingly, the Braves are uninterested in moving former National League MVP Ronald Acuña Jr., according to MLB Network. It does feel like Atlanta's offense has gone stale and that they could move a core piece, but it won't be Acuña, nor is it likely to be Drake Baldwin, Michael Harris II, or Matt Olson. Ozzie Albies and Austin Riley are the more obvious players to move. Albies is entering the final season of his wildly team-friendly contract. Riley has six years remaining on his deal, which figures to make a move difficult.

On Monday, Red Sox chief baseball officer Craig Breslow declined to commit to Trevor Story as his starting shortstop next season. "We still have a ton of confidence in Trevor, but any decisions like that are going to take place between Trevor and me far before I speak to anything publicly," Breslow told reporters, including the Boston Globe. Boston has one of the top prospects in the minors in shortstop Franklin Arias, who is very likely to make his debut early next year. Arias could push Story into a utility role or off the team entirely.

Why Joshua Jackson Was Only Dawson's Creek Star Missing From Memoir

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

In Kerr Smith’s new memoir I Don’t Want to Wait: My Life on Dawson’s Creek, Joshua Jackson was not interviewed like this fellow cast—and Smith shared why in an exclusive E! News interview.

Kerr Smith didn’t want to wait to tell his Dawson’s Creek story.

And so, fans of The WB series might notice that while Katie Holmes, Michelle Williams and James Van Der Beek sat for interviews for his new memoir I Don’t Want to Wait: My Life on Dawson’s Creek, Joshua Jackson did not return to Capeside—and that wasn’t Smith’s decision.

“All I want to say is everybody makes a choice about whether or not they want to be part of the project or not,” Smith told E! News in an exclusive interview. “Some did, some didn't.”

And Smith appeared to nod to Jackson’s hesitation to participate in a parenthetical within the book itself, which hit shelves today, Nov. 10, while recounting that scene between Holmes’ Joey and Jackson’s Pacey in the series finale.

While pushing back on Holmes’ admission that she didn’t love her performance, Smith revealed Jackson wasn’t available to share his perspective.

“Allow me to weigh in with my expert analysis. (Yep, I’m now an expert. I mean, have you not heard? I wrote the book on this stuff.),” Smith wrote. “With all due respect, you’re wrong. You positively crushed this scene. Both of you. Right, Josh? Josh? (I’m sure he’ll get back to me any second now.)”

E! News reached out to Jackson’s rep for comment but hasn’t heard back.

But while Smith and Jackson didn’t go down memory lane, or rather creek, together, the Final Destination alum emphasized what it meant to him to revisit series with the rest of the cast and crew.

“I wanted to get everybody else’s memory on it too,” Smith told E!. “I did over two dozen interviews for the book so I wanted to get everybody’s experiences and put it in one place to understand what a great show this is.”

When it came to his conversations with Holmes, the 54-year-old mused that it was easy for them to simply snap right back into the late 1990s, adding, “It’s like we never even lost a step.”

“You make those bonds back then and you just kind of hang on to them,” he added. “It doesn’t matter how many years have gone by.”

And, of course, it was especially sentimental for Smith to get to reminisce with Van Der Beek before his death earlier this year after a three-year battle with colorectal cancer.

“When I did interview James, he was already pretty sick,” Smith noted. “But he was kind enough to give me a few hours and we had an amazing discussion.”

In addition to being a reflection on Dawson’s Creek as a whole, Smith also included a chapter for former cast and crew to share their memories with Van Der Beek following his death.

“I wanted to give everybody that knew him back then a chance to say anything at all if they wanted to,” Smith shared. “I just thought it was really nice. I think it’s something that he would’ve been OK with.”

As he put it, “I think it’s a good way to end the story.”

You can read the entire fantastic, Stephen Spielberg-film worthy story in Smith’s new memoir, and keep reading for even more secrets into the beloved teen drama…

While Fox was first to pick up Dawson's Creek, it subsequently dumped the teen drama.

"I was told they were struggling with Party of Five and they didn't need another one," series creator Kevin Williamson said at the 2015 ATX TV Festival.

But that paved the way for The WB to pick it up two years later.

While Paula Cole's "I Don't Want to Wait" became the iconic Dawson's Creek theme song, producers originally wanted Alanis Morissette's "Hand in My Pocket."

Alas, they couldn't land the rights, what a jagged little pill.

Dawson. Pacey. Joey. Not names you hear every day. So how did Williamson come up with them?

"Dawson came from a real place called Dawson's Creek where we all hung out as kids and partied," he told The Hollywood Reporter in 2018, while Pacey "came from a friend of a friend named Pacey and I'd never met anyone with that name and thought it was a cool name."

And for the show's heroine, Williamson "wanted Josephine as a very girly name that could easily turn into a tomboy name like Joey."

Remember Dawson's infamous "walk the dog" line in the pilot?

Williamson inserted the euphemism because the network would not let them say the word "masturbate" in 1998.

Before Katie Holmes was cast in the role that would make her America's sweetheart-next-door, Blair was this close to landing the role.

"Joe was written to be a tomboy and everyone was coming in being very much a tomboy. We were very close to going with Selma Blair, who was amazing," Williamson told THR. "She read it very tough, with a lot of heart."

However, once he saw Holmes on tape, with "those two big eyes," Williamson knew they had their Joey.

Williamson liked Joshua Jackson so much, he didn't care what role the Mighty Ducks alum played.

"I fell in love with Josh Jackson because he could read any role, Dawson or Pacey," he told THR. "But something wasn't complete and that's when the network said they didn't see Josh as Dawson, and rightfully so. So, I went, 'OK, he's Pacey,' because I knew I wanted him in the show no matter what."

Charlie Hunnam Auditioned, Won Role of Husband

An 18-year-old Charlie Hunnam read for the WB drama in 1999 and, while he didn't land the part, he did meet Katherine Towne at his audition.

They tied the knot in Las Vegas three weeks later and divorced in 2002, "three terrible, painful, expensive years later," as Hunnam put it to the Associated Press in 2017.

Dawson's Creek became so popular that American Eagle inked a deal in 2000 to outfit the actors.

The characters wore mostly AE clothes throughout season three, and the cast appeared in ad campaigns and promos for the clothing line, helping drive the company's net income above $105 million in 2001.

When James Van Der Beek was first cast, the studio wanted him to change his hair.

"We found an ad for The Devil's Own, the movie with Brad Pitt," he recalled to The Daily Beast in 2012. "They said: 'What about Brad Pitt's haircut?' That's how I got my season one haircut."

Holmes dated Jackson early in the show's run, before Pacey and Joey realized they opposite-of-hated each other.

"I'm just going to say that I met somebody last year," Holmes told Rolling Stone in 1998, not denying that the somebody was Jackson. "I fell in love, I had my first love, and it was something so incredible and indescribable that I will treasure it always. And that I feel so fortunate because he's now one of my best friends. It's weird, it's almost like a Dawson-and-Joey type thing now."

Plus one more nugget: "He's been in the business so long," she added, "and he's really helped me. I respect him as a friend and a professional."

When Kerr Smith first signed on to join the show as Joey's new boyfriend, he had no idea the character would eventually come out and be part of the first-ever gay kiss on TV.

"I always knew I wanted Jack to come out of the closet, but I didn't even tell [Smith]," Williamson said at the ATX TV Festival. "Let the audience love him, then let's have him come out of the closet and have Joey have to deal with that—and then that would eventually drive her back to [Dawson]."

More poignantly, Williamson has said that every character on the show inherited traits from him, including Jack.

“Every single character has a trait of me in them,” he told Entertainment Weekly in 2018. “I had just, in my 20s, gone through the coming-out process and had told my parents I was gay. I had taken that whole journey, and I wanted a character on the show to represent that journey and to represent that side of me."

Jack's coming out episode, cowritten by Williamson and producer Greg Berlanti, was inspired by true events.

"A friend of mine had a story that I brought in that he got outed accidentally," Berlanti told EW, "because he wrote a love poem that it was so clear to everyone else that it was about a guy.”

Michelle Williams and Busy Philipps' best-friendship, which first blossomed on Dawson's Creek, is still going strong to this day.

“I’m so in love with her,” Williams told People of her bestie at a 2016 screening of Manchester by the Sea, one of many red carpet date nights for the besties. “She’s proof that the love of your life does not have to be a man! That’s the love of my life right there.”

Philipps is also godmother to Williams' daughter Matilda Ledger, who was born in 2005.

Dawson finally losing his virginity was such a big show moment that Van Der Beek brought a very special gift to set the day the scene was filmed.

"I remember bringing champagne for the crew—Dawson finally lost his virginity!" he told The Daily Beast.

Before starring on One Tree Hill, Chad Michael Murray had an arc on Dawson's Creek playing Charlie, a love interest for Jen and Joey.

But while Charlie got around, Murray didn't seem to make a lot of friends during his first stint in North Carolinav (OTH later filmed there).

During a Paley panel in 2009, Philipps called him "a douche," later saying, "Don't worry. I'm not real worried about burning bridges with CMM."

Van Der Beek added, "He's actually come a long way." Post-event, Philipps tweeted, "Just finished the dawson's creek panel. Maybe I was too harsh on chadM2… Nah."

Murray, meanwhile, hasn't stirred the pot, saying in 2015 that he learned a lot from the experience.

“I had the opportunity to learn from people who had been doing it for a couple of years longer than I was when I walked into the Dawson’s cast,” he told Cosmpolitan.com in 2015. “You know, Michelle and James and Katie and Josh—they really kind of guided me, because they’d already been around for five, six years, maybe longer. And if they had the time, they were showing me the ropes. I had no idea what lighting was, I had no idea about a lot of the technical aspects of what we do, and so I’m grateful for that experience.”

Just a sampling of the famous faces, ranging from the already familiar to future huge stars, who appeared on Dawson's Creek over the years: Seth Rogen, Jane Lynch, Julie Bowen, Scott Foley, Jensen Ackles, Hilarie Burton, Rachel Leigh Cook, Ali Larter and Oliver Hudson.

Young Americans was a short-lived spinoff centering on Pacey's friend Will Krudski (Rodney Scott) and his boarding school classmates at Rawley Academy.

Also starring Ian Somerhalder and Kate Bosworth, it was canceled after just one season.

The Show Almost Ended With Joey Picking Dawson

The show's main love triangle officially disassembled when Joey chose Pacey in the 2003 series finale.

But it almost didn't turn out that way, as Williamson first envisioned Dawson getting the girl. But halfway through writing the finale, Williamson realized that a happy ending for that couple wasn't "what the show was set to be."

"I wanted it to be a twist on the teen genre but also wanted it to be surprising, honest and real and say something about soul mates and what soul mates can be," he explained at the ATX TV Festival. "That's why we did it that way. When you left the show in that last moment, they're a family and everyone got what they wanted. There was fulfillment and they were all happy."

At the last minute, he changed his mind and the rest is TV history. But Williamson admitted his mom, who played for Team Dawson, was not happy.

In the finale, which jumped ahead five years, the group had to deal with the devastating loss of Jen.

"Dealing with the death of one of their own was the final thing that thrust them into adulthood forever. Dawson's Creek was a coming-of-age story and that was the idea behind that ending," Williamson explained of the decision. "That's why we killed Jen, because I wanted them to deal with a death of one their own as that final lesson."

Some of the executive producers who get their start on Dawson's?

Julie Plec (who went on to gift us with The Vampire Diaries and The Originals), Berlanti (Everwood, Arrow, The Flash, Riverdale...the list goes on and on, and that doesn't even include his movies), Rob Thomas (Veronica Mars and iZombie), Jenny Bicks (Sex and the City), Anna Fricke (Being Human), Dana Baratta (Jessica Jones) and many more.

‘Chucky’ Creator Don Mancini Signs With Independent Artist Group

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Don Mancini, the writer, director and producer best known as the creative force behind the iconic Chucky horror franchise, has signed with Independent Artist Group for representation.

Mancini introduced audiences to the murderous doll Chucky in the original 1988 Child’s Play. He has since written every sequel in the original film franchise and directed several installments, including Seed of Chucky, Curse of Chucky and 2017’s Cult of Chucky.

Over nearly four decades, the franchise has become a pop culture phenomenon, spawning not only multiple films, but also a television series and an extensive global merchandising business. Mancini himself brought the franchise into television, serving as creator and showrunner of Chucky, which aired for three seasons on Syfy and USA Network and introduced the killer doll to a new generation of fans.

Other television credits for Mancini include serving as a supervising producer on Syfy’s horror anthology Channel Zero, as a producer on NBC’s Hannibal and as a writer on HBO’s Tales From the Crypt. His feature writing credits also include the cult horror Cellar Dweller. Watch on Deadline

At IAG, Mancini will focus on developing the next iteration of the Chucky franchise while also expanding his creative footprint with a slate of original film and television projects; certainly, it feels like we’re overdue for a new Chucky film.

Mancini continues to be represented by attorney Joel VanderKloot of VanderKloot Law.
